{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01FaE6QbFwjFqULZ8vw4dcsh","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01KqACHap1hsBSVB4JrHR2ha","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/supply-use-tables.md","content":"# Supply and Use Tables (SUT) for National Accounts\n\n## Overview\nSupply and Use Tables are fundamental tools in System of National Accounts (SNA 2008/2012) for economic analysis. They show the production, consumption, and trade relationships in an economy.\n\n## Structure\n\n### Supply Table (38x38 for Georgia)\n- **Rows**: Products/commodities (38 categories)\n- **Columns**: Industries/sectors (38 categories)\n- Shows: Who produces what (supply by industry)\n- Key data: Domestic production output by sector\n\n### Use Table (38x38 for Georgia)\n- **Rows**: Products/commodities (38 categories)\n- **Columns**: Industries/sectors + Final demand components\n- Shows: Who uses what and for what purpose\n- Key components:\n  - Intermediate consumption (by industry)\n  - Final consumption (households, government, NPISH)\n  - Gross Fixed Capital Formation (GFCF/Investment)\n  - Exports and Imports\n  - Domestic output usage\n\n## Key Concepts for This Task\n\n### Import Content Share\n- Definition: Percentage of total supply that comes from imports vs. domestic production\n- Formula: `Import_Content = Total_Imports / Total_Supply`\n- Used in: Demand multiplier adjustments for open economies\n- For 38x38 tables: Calculate across all products and industries\n\n### Calculation Steps for SUT Analysis\n1. Extract import values from USE table (import row)\n2. Extract domestic supply values from SUPPLY table\n3. Calculate total supply (domestic + imports)\n4. Compute import content share for each sector/product\n5. Weight by investment composition to get overall import content\n\n## Georgia Statistical Authority (GeoStat)\n\n### Website Structure\n- Main: https://www.geostat.ge/\n- Supply and Use Tables: In \"National Accounts\" section\n- File format: Usually Excel or downloadable CSV\n- Latest available year: Check GeoStat for most recent SUT publication\n\n### Accessing the SUT\n1. Navigate to \"National Accounts\" section\n2. Look for \"Supply and Use Tables\" or \"Symmetric Input-Output Tables\"\n3. Download 38x38 product×industry format\n4. Tables typically labeled: SUPPLY sheet and USE sheet\n5. Keep original sheet names unchanged for traceability\n\n## Excel Integration for SUT Calc Sheet\n\n### Column Mapping (C-H)\n- **Column C**: Product/Commodity name (from SUT)\n- **Column D**: Domestic supply (from SUPPLY table)\n- **Column E**: Import values (from USE table)\n- **Column F**: Total supply (=C+D, using formula)\n- **Column G**: Import share (=E/F, using formula)\n- **Column H**: Weight/Relevance (for investment composition)\n\n### Cell C46: Import Content Share\n- Definition: Weighted average import share across all products\n- Used in demand-side multiplier calculations\n- Formula: `=SUMPRODUCT(import_share, investment_weights) / SUM(investment_weights)`\n- Or simplified: `=Total_Imports / Total_Supply`\n\n## Notes\n- SUT tables are updated annually, typically with 1-2 year lag\n- Ensure consistency between base years used in different tables\n- Import content varies significantly by sector (e.g., high in manufacturing, lower in services)\n- For small open economy analysis: Georgia typically has high import content (0.4-0.6 range)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":858,"cache_read_input_tokens":19091,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":858,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a894a6c2-6052-4348-a679-14280634137d","uuid":"23762f85-8c3d-44b1-a2e7-a8d006e63cf2"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_019bd8FL8Sd6HCCRZ4bbNLoD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01Cb2LxSxm1gARwiDVPZ9DyC","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/excel-macro-accounting.md","content":"# Excel-Based Macro Accounting Framework (Demand-Side Analysis)\n\n## Overview\nDemand-side macro accounting in Excel models the economic impact of shocks (like investment spending) through multiplier effects, considering an open economy structure.\n\n## Key Components\n\n### 1. National Accounts (NA) Sheet Structure\n\n#### Part A: Baseline and Shock Projections\n- **Row labels**: Economic categories (GDP, consumption, investment, exports, imports, etc.)\n- **Column C**: Baseline year (e.g., 2025)\n- **Columns D-T**: Projection years (2026-2043)\n- **Formula structure**: Year-on-year calculations with deflators\n\n#### Part B: Key Calculations\n- Real GDP = Consumption + Investment + Government Spending + Net Exports\n- Nominal GDP = Real GDP × GDP Deflator\n- Investment Deflator = GDP Deflator (as per task hint)\n\n### 2. Demand-Side Multiplier Framework\n\n#### Multiplier Concept\n- **Demand Multiplier**: How much total GDP increases for each unit of investment\n- **Formula**: ΔY = m × ΔI\n  - ΔY = change in GDP\n  - m = demand multiplier\n  - ΔI = investment shock\n\n#### Multiplier Adjustment for Open Economies\n- **Closed economy**: m = 1/(1-c) where c = marginal propensity to consume\n  - Typical range: 1.5-2.5\n- **Small open economy**: m is smaller due to imports leaking\n  - **Formula**: m_open = m_closed × (1 - import_content_share)\n  - Georgia example: m = 0.8 (baseline scenario)\n  - Alternative: m = 1.0 (Scenario 2)\n\n#### Import Content Integration\n- **Import Content Share**: Percentage of additional demand met by imports\n- **Usage**: Reduces domestic multiplier effect\n- **Cell reference**: C46 from SUT Calc sheet (typically 0.4-0.6 for Georgia)\n\n### 3. Investment Shock Modeling\n\n#### Bell-Shape Allocation (Project Allocation Column)\n- **Concept**: Investment spending peaks in middle years, tapers at start and end\n- **Time horizon**: 8 years (2026-2033)\n- **Pattern**:\n  - Year 1-2: Ramping up (e.g., 10-15% of annual allocation)\n  - Year 3-5: Peak (e.g., 20-25% of annual allocation)\n  - Year 6-8: Tapering (e.g., 15-10% of annual allocation)\n- **Excel formula**: Can use normal distribution formula or manual percentage allocation\n- **Total**: Must sum to 100% (or 1.0 if decimal)\n- **Example bell curve**:\n  ```\n  Year 1: 0.08\n  Year 2: 0.15\n  Year 3: 0.22\n  Year 4: 0.25\n  Year 5: 0.22\n  Year 6: 0.05\n  Year 7: 0.02\n  Year 8: 0.01\n  ```\n\n### 4. Key Assumptions Section (Cells D30-D33)\n\nTypical layout:\n- **D30**: Demand multiplier (0.8 for baseline, 1.0 for Scenario 2)\n- **D31**: Exchange rate (2.746 Lari/USD - fixed)\n- **D32**: Import content share (from C46, adjustable in Scenario 3 to 0.5)\n- **D33**: Investment deflator (link to GDP deflator)\n\n### 5. Excel Formula Patterns\n\n#### Deflator Projection\n```excel\n=Deflator_Previous_Year * (1 + Avg_Deflator_Growth_Rate)\n```\n\n#### Nominal to Real Conversion\n```excel\n=Nominal_Value / (Deflator / 100) [if deflator is index]\nor\n=Nominal_Value / Deflator [if deflator is already factor]\n```\n\n#### Multiplier Effect on GDP\n```excel\n=Base_GDP + (Investment_Shock * Demand_Multiplier * (1 - Import_Content_Share))\n```\n\n#### Investment Shock by Year\n```excel\n=Total_Investment * Project_Allocation_Percentage * (1 + Real_GDP_Growth_Rate)\n```\n\n## Scenario Structure\n\n### Baseline Scenario\n- Demand multiplier: 0.8 (typical for small open economy)\n- Import content: From SUT calculation (C46)\n- Investment: Bell-shaped allocation, 6.5B USD over 8 years\n\n### Scenario 2\n- Demand multiplier: 1.0 (increased multiplier)\n- Everything else same as baseline\n- Interpretation: Higher domestic content or lower import substitution\n\n### Scenario 3\n- Demand multiplier: 0.8 (baseline)\n- Import content: 0.5 (50% of demand met by imports)\n- Interpretation: Different import content assumption\n\n## Output Interpretation\n\n### Key Metrics to Calculate\n- **Year-by-year GDP impact**: Additional GDP generated\n- **Cumulative impact**: Total additional GDP over project period\n- **Multiplier effect**: Ratio of total impact to direct investment\n- **Sectoral impacts**: If disaggregated by sector\n\n## Notes\n- All formulas must be dynamic (no hardcoded numbers)\n- Use cell references for all assumptions\n- Update assumptions section to see automatic recalculation\n- Maintain separate scenario tables for comparison\n- Keep baseline scenario visible for reference\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":912,"cache_read_input_tokens":19949,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":912,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a894a6c2-6052-4348-a679-14280634137d","uuid":"b9c741a3-6c58-4252-9cf3-0a751435c2a7"}

National Accounts (NA) Sheet Structure\n\n#### Part A: Baseline and Shock Projections\n- **Row labels**: Economic categories (GDP, consumption, investment, exports, imports, etc.)\n- **Column C**: Baseline year (e.g., 2025)\n- **Columns D-T**: Projection years (2026-2043)\n- **Formula structure**: Year-on-year calculations with deflators\n\n#### Part B: Key Calculations\n- Real GDP = Consumption + Investment + Government Spending + Net Exports\n- Nominal GDP = Real GDP × GDP Deflator\n- Investment Deflator = GDP Deflator (as per task hint)\n\n### 2. Demand-Side Multiplier Framework\n\n#### Multiplier Concept\n- **Demand Multiplier**: How much total GDP increases for each unit of investment\n- **Formula**: ΔY = m × ΔI\n  - ΔY = change in GDP\n  - m = demand multiplier\n  - ΔI = investment shock\n\n#### Multiplier Adjustment for Open Economies\n- **Closed economy**: m = 1/(1-c) where c = marginal propensity to consume\n  - Typical range: 1.5-2.5\n- **Small open economy**: m is smaller due to imports leaking\n  - **Formula**: m_open = m_closed × (1 - import_content_share)\n  - Georgia example: m = 0.8 (baseline scenario)\n  - Alternative: m = 1.0 (Scenario 2)\n\n#### Import Content Integration\n- **Import Content Share**: Percentage of additional demand met by imports\n- **Usage**: Reduces domestic multiplier effect\n- **Cell reference**: C46 from SUT Calc sheet (typically 0.4-0.6 for Georgia)\n\n### 3. Investment Shock Modeling\n\n#### Bell-Shape Allocation (Project Allocation Column)\n- **Concept**: Investment spending peaks in middle years, tapers at start and end\n- **Time horizon**: 8 years (2026-2033)\n- **Pattern**:\n  - Year 1-2: Ramping up (e.g., 10-15% of annual allocation)\n  - Year 3-5: Peak (e.g., 20-25% of annual allocation)\n  - Year 6-8: Tapering (e.g., 15-10% of annual allocation)\n- **Excel formula**: Can use normal distribution formula or manual percentage allocation\n- **Total**: Must sum to 100% (or 1.0 if decimal)\n- **Example bell curve**:\n  ```\n  Year 1: 0.08\n  Year 2: 0.15\n  Year 3: 0.22\n  Year 4: 0.25\n  Year 5: 0.22\n  Year 6: 0.05\n  Year 7: 0.02\n  Year 8: 0.01\n  ```\n\n### 4. Key Assumptions Section (Cells D30-D33)\n\nTypical layout:\n- **D30**: Demand multiplier (0.8 for baseline, 1.0 for Scenario 2)\n- **D31**: Exchange rate (2.746 Lari/USD - fixed)\n- **D32**: Import content share (from C46, adjustable in Scenario 3 to 0.5)\n- **D33**: Investment deflator (link to GDP deflator)\n\n### 5. Excel Formula Patterns\n\n#### Deflator Projection\n```excel\n=Deflator_Previous_Year * (1 + Avg_Deflator_Growth_Rate)\n```\n\n#### Nominal to Real Conversion\n```excel\n=Nominal_Value / (Deflator / 100) [if deflator is index]\nor\n=Nominal_Value / Deflator [if deflator is already factor]\n```\n\n#### Multiplier Effect on GDP\n```excel\n=Base_GDP + (Investment_Shock * Demand_Multiplier * (1 - Import_Content_Share))\n```\n\n#### Investment Shock by Year\n```excel\n=Total_Investment * Project_Allocation_Percentage * (1 + Real_GDP_Growth_Rate)\n```\n\n## Scenario Structure\n\n### Baseline Scenario\n- Demand multiplier: 0.8 (typical for small open economy)\n- Import content: From SUT calculation (C46)\n- Investment: Bell-shaped allocation, 6.5B USD over 8 years\n\n### Scenario 2\n- Demand multiplier: 1.0 (increased multiplier)\n- Everything else same as baseline\n- Interpretation: Higher domestic content or lower import substitution\n\n### Scenario 3\n- Demand multiplier: 0.8 (baseline)\n- Import content: 0.5 (50% of demand met by imports)\n- Interpretation: Different import content assumption\n\n## Output Interpretation\n\n### Key Metrics to Calculate\n- **Year-by-year GDP impact**: Additional GDP generated\n- **Cumulative impact**: Total additional GDP over project period\n- **Multiplier effect**: Ratio of total impact to direct investment\n- **Sectoral impacts**: If disaggregated by sector\n\n## Notes\n- All formulas must be dynamic (no hardcoded numbers)\n- Use cell references for all assumptions\n- Update assumptions section to see automatic recalculation\n- Maintain separate scenario tables for comparison\n- Keep baseline scenario visible for reference\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Change only assumption cells\n   - All other cells update automatically\n   - Easy to compare scenarios side-by-side\n\n## Excel Features for This Task\n\n### Useful Functions\n- `SUM()`: Sum range of cells\n- `SUMPRODUCT()`: Weighted sum\n- `AVERAGE()`: Calculate average\n- `IF()`: Conditional logic\n- `INDEX/MATCH`: Dynamic cell lookup\n- `VLOOKUP`: Vertical lookup (if organizing SUT data)\n- `ROW()/COLUMN()`: Dynamic reference\n\n### Data Organization Tips\n1. The demand-side approach tracks how spending generates GDP.\n\n## Demand-Side GDP Identity\n\n### Core Formula\n```\nGDP = C + I + G + (X - M)\n\nWhere:\nC  = Final consumption (households + government + NPISH)\nI  = Gross Fixed Capital Formation (Investment)\nG  = Government spending (sometimes included in C)\nX  = Exports\nM  = Imports\nX-M = Net exports\n```\n\n### Extensions for Economic Modelling\n```\nNominal GDP = Real GDP × GDP Deflator (as index, e.g., 100 = base year)\nor\nReal GDP (current prices) = Nominal GDP / (Deflator/100)\n```\n\n## Key Variables for Georgia Investment Analysis\n\n### Baseline Data (from WEO)\n- **Real GDP** (constant prices, millions or billions USD/Lari)\n- **Nominal GDP** (current prices)\n- **GDP Deflator** (index or growth rate)\n- **Real GDP Growth Rate** (% annually)\n- **Population** (if needed for per capita analysis)\n\n### Investment Shock Components\n- **Total Investment**: 6.5 billion USD\n- **Project Duration**: 8 years (2026-2033)\n- **Annual Allocation**: Bell-shaped distribution\n- **Investment Deflator**: GDP deflator (per task hint)\n\n### Multiplier Components\n\n#### Direct Effect\n- Investment spending in Year t\n\n#### Indirect Effects\n- Induced consumption from income generated\n- Supply-chain purchases (intermediate consumption)\n- Re-spending of earned income\n\n#### Leakage Effects (Reducing Multiplier)\n- **Imports**: High for small open economies like Georgia\n- **Taxes**: Withdrawn to government\n- **Savings**: Not re-spent in current period\n\n#### Import Content Adjustment\n- Small open economy assumption\n- Base multiplier (e.g., 0.8) already reflects typical import content\n- Scenario 3 explores alternative import assumption (0.5)\n\n## GDP Deflator Concepts\n\n### Definition\n- Price level indicator showing cost of goods/services produced\n- Index: Base year typically = 100\n- Growth rate: Percentage annual change\n\n### Calculation from IMF WEO\n1. Extract nominal GDP and real GDP from WEO\n2. Calculate deflator: Nominal GDP / Real GDP × 100\n3. Calculate growth rate: (Deflator_t - Deflator_t-1) / Deflator_t-1\n4. Average last 4 years of growth\n5. Project forward using fixed average growth rate\n\n### Application to Investment\n- **Task instruction**: Use GDP deflator as investment deflator\n- Rationale: Investment goods typically track general price level\n- Formula: Investment_Nominal = Investment_Real × (Deflator / 100)\n\n## National Accounts Sheet (NA) Structure\n\n### Section 1: Historical Baseline and WEO Projections\n```\nRow 1:        Category / 2024 / 2025 / 2026 / ... / 2043\nRow 2:        Real GDP Growth Rate (%)\nRow 3:        Nominal GDP\nRow 4:        GDP Deflator (index)\nRow 5:        Real GDP\nRow 6-10:     Other indicators (population, etc.)\n```\n\n### Section 2: Investment Shock Parameters\n```\nRow 15:       Investment Shock (USD millions or billions)\nRow 16:       Year Allocation (%)\nRow 17:       Project Allocation (bell curve)\nRow 18:       Investment Shock Distribution\n```\n\n### Section 3: Scenario Assumptions\n```\nRow 30:       Demand Multiplier\nRow 31:       Exchange Rate\nRow 32:       Import Content Share\nRow 33:       Investment Deflator\n```\n\n### Section 4: Results (For Each Scenario)\n```\nRow 35:       Baseline GDP (from WEO)\nRow 36:       Additional GDP from Investment Shock\nRow 37:       Total GDP (Baseline + Shock)\nRow 38:       Real GDP Growth Rate (with shock)\nRow 39:       Multiplier Effect (Total Impact / Investment)\nRow 40:       Cumulative Impact (if needed)\n```\n\n## Specific Formulas for Georgia Analysis\n\n### 1. Deflator Projection (for years without WEO forecast)\n```excel\nAverage_Deflator_Growth = AVERAGE(Deflator_Growth_Last_4_Years)\nDeflator_2027 = Deflator_2026 * (1 + Average_Deflator_Growth)\nDeflator_2028 = Deflator_2027 * (1 + Average_Deflator_Growth)\n... and so on until 2043\n```\n\n### 2. Bell-Shape Investment Allocation\n```\nAssumption: 8-year project with peak in middle years\nOptions:\nA) Manual allocation (recommended for clarity)\n   Year 1: 8% / Year 2: 12% / Year 3: 18% / Year 4: 20%\n   Year 5: 20% / Year 6: 15% / Year 7: 5% / Year 8: 2%\n\nB) Excel formula (using NORM.DIST for normal distribution)\n   =NORM.DIST(relative_position, mean=0.5, std_dev=0.2, FALSE)\n```\n\n### 3. Annual Investment Shock\n```excel\nAnnual_Investment_USD = Total_Investment * Project_Allocation_Year_t\nAnnual_Investment_Lari = Annual_Investment_USD * Exchange_Rate\nAnnual_Investment_Nominal = Annual_Investment_Lari / (Deflator/100)\n```\n\n### 4. GDP Impact Calculation\n```excel\nBaseline_GDP_Real_t = (baseline from WEO or calculated)\nAdditional_GDP_t = Investment_Shock_t * Demand_Multiplier\nTotal_GDP_Real_t = Baseline_GDP_Real_t + Additional_GDP_t\nTotal_GDP_Nominal_t = Total_GDP_Real_t * (Deflator/100)\n```\n\n### 5. Multiplier Adjustment for Imports\n```excel\nBase_Multiplier = 0.8 (or 1.0 for Scenario 2)\nImport_Content = C46 (or 0.5 for Scenario 3)\nAdjusted_Effect = Investment_Shock * Base_Multiplier * (1 - Import_Content)\n```\n\n## Data Linkage\n\n### From WEO_Data Sheet to NA Sheet\n```\nNA!C2 = WEO_Data!C3 (link real GDP growth rate)\nNA!C3 = WEO_Data!C2 (link nominal GDP)\nNA!C4 = WEO_Data!C4 (link GDP deflator)\n... and so on for other years\n```\n\n### From SUT Calc Sheet to NA Sheet\n```\nNA!D32 = SUT_Calc!C46 (import content share)\n```\n\n## Scenario Comparison\n\nAfter building all three scenarios, create comparison showing:\n- Baseline scenario results\n- Scenario 2 (higher multiplier) results\n- Scenario 3 (different import content) results\n- Differences and % deviations from baseline\n\n## Notes\n- Demand-side approach: Focus on spending and income\n- Small open economy: High import content, lower multipliers\n- Georgia context: Tourism, agriculture, and services important sectors\n- Multi-year project: Phased implementation affects timing of impacts\n- Exchange rate fixed: Simplifies analysis (real depreciation not modeled)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1343,"cache_read_input_tokens":22305,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1343,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a894a6c2-6052-4348-a679-14280634137d","uuid":"59f212c3-c0b3-4e9e-a6a1-a2806a146019"}
